**the finding, re-verified on disk 2026-08-14** (⚠️ **anchor on quoted text, not line numbers**, and ⚠️ **re-verify every item rather than reasoning from this row**): **(1)** `claude/fkit-claude.sh`'s header documents the second session lock as a `PreToolUse` hook (`skill-ownership-hook.sh`, task 43 / [ADR-018](../knowledge-base/decisions/adr-018-pretooluse-skill-ownership-hook-replaces-consult-skills-exception-list.md)) that denies *"any `Skill` call whose REAL invoking agent's role doesn't own it, per skills_for_role() — at any spawn depth"*; **(2)** the hook **matches on `Skill` and nothing else** — its `case "$tool_name" in` block allows `Skill` and denies everything else as *"unexpected tool_name … reached a hook registered for Skill only"*, its own comment recording that this is defensive because *"the hook is registered with matcher `Skill` in `build_settings()`"*, so ⭐ **the hook is NEVER CONSULTED for a `Bash` call at all — not "consulted and allowed", NOT CONSULTED**, a distinction the recommendation should state precisely; **(3)** role procedures **also ship as shell scripts** — a `find` over `claude/skills/` for `*.sh` returns **exactly three** (⚠️ re-run it, the set will grow): `claude/skills/fkit-heal/check.sh` (⭐ **never writes, in any branch**), `claude/skills/fkit-heal/repair.sh` (⛔ **WRITES — `apply` replaces files in place**) and `claude/skills/fkit-status/dashboard.sh` (renders a board, no write) — ⚠️ **and note what that set shows which the request did not: ALL THREE ARE PRODUCER-OWNED** (`fkit-heal` and `fkit-status` both sit in the `producer)` arm of `skills_for_role()`), so the live exposure, if it is one, is *"any Bash-capable role can run the PRODUCER's scripts"*, ⭐ **a sharper and narrower statement than "roles can run each other's scripts" — decide against the real surface, not the abstract one**; **(4)** invoking one is a **`Bash`** call, not a `Skill` call — all three are mode **`644`, NOT executable**, and are run as `bash <path>`, `dashboard.sh`'s header recording why (*"it rides a GitHub tarball + `cp -R` chain that does not guarantee the exec bit … Invoking through `bash` sidesteps"* it), ⚠️ **so the Bash-not-Skill shape is NOT incidental — it is how these scripts are ALWAYS run, including by their owning role**; **(5)** [ADR-022](../knowledge-base/decisions/adr-022-tools-unrestricted-except-adversarial-reviewer.md) relaxed the tool allowlist for the six Claude-side roles and ⚠️ **the adversarial reviewer's surviving `tools: Read, Grep, Glob, Bash, Skill` line includes `Bash` too**, so ⛔ **all seven roles can invoke all three scripts and the ownership hook never sees it**; **(6)** ⚠️ **the scripts carry NO caller-identity guard** — a grep of all three for `agent_type`, `FKIT_ROLE`, `skills_for_role` and `caller` returns **only comment matches about the calling CODE**, never a check on the invoking ROLE (⚠️ **the driver did not check this; this brief did, as a single-turn observation — RE-VERIFY IT**), and the many guards `repair.sh` **does** carry (path validation, an `ai-agents/wiki-vault/` refusal, symlink and hard-link refusals, eligibility re-check, apply-time freshness re-check, written-bytes re-hash) all constrain **WHAT** may be written, ⛔ **never WHO asked**; **(7) ⚠️ THE SHARPEST POINT — the consent gate is PROSE-SIDE BY CONSTRUCTION**: `repair.sh`'s own header states *"the LLM's only jobs are to PRESENT the proposal verbatim and collect the owner's consent; this script computes what would change and applies exactly what was approved"* and `apply` *"reads approved `item` lines VERBATIM on stdin"* — ⭐ **nothing in the script verifies a human was ever asked**, it verifies only that the items are eligible, unchanged and safely writable, ⛔ **so the question is not only "can the wrong role run this" but "does anything MECHANICAL stand between `propose` and `apply`"**: ⚠️ **establish this EMPIRICALLY in a throwaway copy, NEVER the live tree** — ⛔ **do not present a reading of the source as if it were an execution** — ⚠️⚠️ **and weigh the honest counter first: this may be the ALREADY-ACCEPTED posture of [ADR-039](../knowledge-base/decisions/adr-039-consent-gated-structure-repair-licensed-adr-015-invariant-unchanged.md)** rather than a new gap, the consent obligation having been **deliberately** placed in the LLM's prose by ADR-017's deterministic-mechanics-in-scripts split — ⛔ **if so, say so and close it; do NOT re-open ADR-039 by the back door**; **⭐ THE CASE THAT THIS IS DELIBERATE, WHICH MUST BE STATED FAIRLY BEFORE IT IS ARGUED AGAINST**: ADR-018 locks the **procedure**, not the files on disk — every clause of its Decision is about the `Skill` tool, and its §5 accepts a residual (a foreign skill stays **visible** in the `/` menu) reasoning that *"the properties that matter — a coder cannot **run** the reviewer's procedure, at any depth — are what the hook protects"*, ⚠️ **so whether "the procedure" means the SKILL.md instructions or ALSO the bytes those instructions shell out to is THE question, and ADR-018's text does not obviously settle it either way** — ⛔ **do not paraphrase it, QUOTE it**; **⭐⭐ AND ADR-022 §4 IS THE CRUX, CUTTING BOTH WAYS** — verbatim: *"The skill lockdown is unchanged and out of scope … This ADR relaxes **tools (generic capabilities)**, not **skills (named, role-owned procedures)** … the cheap, meaningful separation (a role can't run another's procedure) stays; the blunt one (a role can't hold a generic tool) goes"* — on one reading `bash repair.sh apply` **is** running another role's procedure with a generic tool; on the other the script is **mechanics** rather than procedure and the sentence was only ever about the `Skill` tool it names: ⛔ **a recommendation that does not engage this paragraph DIRECTLY has not done the task**; **⚠️ THE PRESSURE ON THE "BY DESIGN" READING, TO BE ENGAGED NOT WAVED THROUGH** — ADR-018 **§6 rejected prose-only enforcement** as *"defeatable by prompt injection or a simply confused subagent"*, citing ADR-012's evidence of *a spawned `fkit-lead` stopped only by prose*, ⚠️ **and prose is EXACTLY what is left on the bash path today**: each SKILL.md carries a `## ⛔ Owner: the **producer**` banner (verified present in **both** `claude/skills/fkit-heal/SKILL.md` and `claude/skills/fkit-status/SKILL.md`), ⛔ **and an agent invoking the script directly need never open SKILL.md to see it** — ⚠️ whether that is the same hazard ADR-018 rejected or a different and acceptable one is a **judgement to make explicitly**; ⭐ **and `repair.sh`'s WRITE capability may change the answer**: `check.sh` never writes so the benign *"read-only shortcut"* reading `0303` reaches for covers it comfortably, ⛔ **but it does NOT cover `repair.sh`** — ⚠️ **say whether the answer is the same for both or SPLITS**, a split answer (read-only scripts free, writing scripts not) being legitimate ⛔ **only if the boundary it draws is stated in a form something could actually ENFORCE**, since *"read-only"* is not a property the hook can see; **(ii)** does `repair.sh`'s write capability change the answer versus `check.sh`'s read-only one — answer for **both** and say whether they split; **(iii)** **is it reachable in practice, or does something else stop it?** ⚠️ **VERIFY, DO NOT ASSUME** — covering the scripts' own guards (⛔ re-verify finding 6 rather than taking this row's word), whether `apply` can be driven from `propose` with no human in between, and whether anything **outside** the scripts (a permission prompt, a settings rule, a hook on `Bash`) intervenes — ⭐ **a NEGATIVE result here is a first-class answer**: "no gap, here is why" is exactly the record the owner asked for; **(iv)** **if it is a real gap, the options PRICED not listed** — at minimum a `PreToolUse` matcher on `Bash` (⚠️ price the false-positive surface honestly: the hook would have to **parse a shell command line**, a harder problem than reading a `skill` field out of JSON, ⚠️ **and ADR-018's fail-OPEN hazard applies to every new code path**), guards **inside** the scripts (⚠️ price the fact that a script has no reliable way to learn its caller's role — say so if that is what you find), and **accept-and-document** (⚠️ price what documenting costs and **where it lives** — an ADR, a SKILL.md line, `architecture.md`) — ⛔ **the brief picks none and the recommendation must not pre-empt the owner's ruling**; ⭐ **and the workaround is six weeks old** — of **29** `adversarial-prompt` files in `.fkit/tmp/`, **28 are already task-keyed** (2026-07-29 → 2026-09-10), so the convention is in real use and **lives in no skill**; ⛔ **the failure is SILENT** — Codex returns a confident, diff-grounded findings list on the wrong diff, with no exit code, no empty output and no auth error, so **none of the skills' graceful-degradation branches ever fires** and the `Coverage:` field truthfully reports a completed pass; ⭐ **nothing detects it** — no test, no hook, no ledger field: this instance was caught by a reviewer's own vigilance and by nothing else; ⚠️ **attribution, both halves** — the ship-loop driver caused this instance by scheduling two reviews in parallel, ⛔ **but the defect is in the skill**: the procedure names the path as a literal, offers no safe way to run two reviews at once, and **nowhere warns against it**; **fix ruled: a task-keyed path** — recommended `.fkit/tmp/<key>-adversarial-prompt.md` with a round suffix where a round exists, `<key>` derived from the **ledger key `fkit-stateful-review` already resolves** rather than a second invented rule, plus one line of hazard text at each assemble step; ⛔ **NOT a lock, mutex or PID file** — that is a different decision needing its own ruling; ⛔ no change to review semantics, coverage states, degradation branches or the ledger schema; ⛔ **no `ai-agents/knowledge-base/` edit** — `architecture.md`, `adr-016` and `adr-042` carry stale mentions of the fixed path and amending them is the **architect's**, flag them in the hand-off;
